## Activity

    $ cat ethglobal/README.md README.md
    # Veil — Private Batched Swaps on Ethereum Sepolia
    
    **Veil** is a working demonstration of *confidential DeFi*: users swap one token
    for another through a batched auction where **what you traded, how much, which
    way, and what you received are all encrypted on-chain** — verified by the Zama
    FHEVM fully homomorphic encryption stack, settled against a real Uniswap v4
    pool, and running live on Ethereum Sepolia.
    
    > ⚠️ **This is a hackathon-grade testnet demo.** It runs on Sepolia with mock
    > tokens. It is not audited, not production-ready, and the demo pair is
    > intentionally 1:1-priced. See [Honest privacy limits](#honest-privacy-limits)
    > for exactly what is and is not hidden.
    
    ---
    
    ## Table of contents
    
    1. [What problem does this solve?](#what-problem-does-this-solve)
    2. [The one-paragraph version](#the-one-paragraph-version)
    3. [How a round works, step by step](#how-a-round-works-step-by-step)
    4. [What is encrypted vs. what is public](#what-is-encrypted-vs-what-is-public)
    5. [The privacy design decisions](#the-privacy-design-decisions)
    6. [Architecture](#architecture)
    7. [Live deployment (Sepolia)](#live-deployment-sepolia)
    8. [Using the app](#using-the-app)
    9. [The keeper daemon](#the-keeper-daemon)
    10. [Contract API reference](#contract-api-reference)
    11. [Running it yourself](#running-it-yourself)
    12. [Testing](#testing)
    13. [Honest privacy limits](#honest-privacy-limits)
    14. [Security and trust assumptions](#security-and-trust-assumptions)
    15. [Repository layout](#repository-layout)
    16. [Known limitations and future work](#known-limitations-and-future-work)
    
    ---
    
    ## What problem does this solve?
    
    On a normal DEX (Uniswap, etc.), every swap is a public event:
    
    - **who** traded (wallet address),
    - **which token pair and direction** they traded,
    - **exactly how much** they bought and sold,
    - **when** they traded.
    
    This is a permanent, publicly queryable record of everyone's trading activity.
    It enables front-running, copy-trading, wallet profiling, and de-anonymization
    of users who thought a pseudonymous address was enough.
    
    Veil explores a different model: **users place orders into a batch, and the
    batch — not the individual — is what the blockchain sees.** Inside the batch,
    every number is a fully homomorphic encryption (FHE) ciphertext: the contract
    can compute on encrypted amounts (sum them, compare them, match them against
    each other) *without ever decrypting them*. Only the minimal aggregate
    information needed to execute one net trade on Uniswap is ever revealed.
    
    ## The one-paragraph version
    
    Users fund a **confidential vault** (ERC-7984 wrapped tokens) once — that
    funding transaction is public, by design. After that, each round they submit an
    **order whose direction (buy A→B or B→A) and size are both encrypted** through
    a single shared entry point that does not reveal which token they are selling.
    When the round's timer closes, the contract **nets all orders against each
    other inside FHE** — matched orders cancel 1:1 and never touch a DEX — and
    only the *net residual* (one encrypted number) plus *which side had the
    surplus* are decrypted via the Zama Gateway. The keeper swaps only that net
    residual on Uniswap v4, and every user's payout is **credited back into their
    encrypted vault balance** — no plaintext payout transaction ever links a
    wallet to an amount received.
    
    ## How a round works, step by step
    
    ```
            ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
            │ 1. FUND (public by design)                                  │
            │    vUSD → wrap → c-vUSD (ERC-7984) → transfer into vault    │
            │    vault balance stored as euint64 — encrypted forever      │
            ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
            │ 2. ORDER (fully encrypted)                                  │
            │    submitOrder(encryptedAmount, encryptedDirection)         │
            │    • direction: euint8  (1 = sell A→B, 2 = sell B→A)        │
            │    • amount:    euint64 (in c-token units)                  │
            │    • one shared entry point for BOTH assets — the path      │
            │      reveals no side; the event reveals no direction/amount │
            ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
            │ 3. CLOSE (encrypted netting inside FHE)                     │
            │    for every pair of opposite-direction orders:             │
            │        m = min(remaining_i, remaining_j)  ← computed on     │
            │        matched_i += m; remaining_i -= m      CIPHERTEXTS     │
            │    → per-order matched / residual stay encrypted            │
            │    → round totals aToB / bToA stay encrypted                │
            ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
            │ 4. DECRYPT THE MINIMUM (the only public decryption)         │
            │    Zama Gateway publicly decrypts exactly two values:       │
            │        net          = |totalA − totalB|   (the residual)    │
            │        surplus side = A or B                                │
            │    Never decrypted: totals, matched, any user's order       │
            ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
            │ 5. SWAP THE NET (public, necessarily)                       │
            │    unwrap net residual → Uniswap v4 swap → wrap output      │
            │    slippage floor enforced on-chain (90% of net)            │
            ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
            │ 6. PAY OUT (confidential)                                   │
            │    creditOrder: payout = matched_i (deficit side, 1:1)      │
            │                = matched_i + residual_i × swapOutput/net    │
            │                  (surplus side — division by the PUBLIC net)│
            │    → credited to the user's ENCRYPTED vault balance         │
            │    → permissionless: anyone can credit anyone               │
            ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
            │ 7. EXIT (optional, public by design)                        │
            │    exit(): unwrap vault balance → plain tokens to wallet    │
            └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    ```
    
    **Why netting matters:** if Alice sells 5 vUSD and Bob sells 5 vUSD→vETH in the
    opposite direction, they match 1:1 *inside the contract* and **nothing at all
    happens on Uniswap**. Only imbalances between the two sides ever trade.
    
    ## What is encrypted vs. what is public
    
    | Information | Status | Notes |
    |---|---|---|
    | Your order **direction** | 🔒 encrypted (euint8) | never decrypted, not even at settlement |
    | Your order **amount** | 🔒 encrypted (euint64) | never decrypted, ever |
    | Your **vault balance** | 🔒 encrypted | only you can decrypt it (userDecrypt) |
    | Your **payout amount** | 🔒 encrypted | credited inside FHE to your vault |
    | Round **directional totals** | 🔒 encrypted | never decrypted — not even the operator sees them |
    | **Matched amount** | 🔒 encrypted | never decrypted |
    | **Net residual** | 🌐 public | it trades on Uniswap — cannot be hidden |
    | **Surplus side** (A or B) | 🌐 public | needed to route the net swap |
    | Round **timing / pair / pool** | 🌐 public | the batch exists and when it closes |
    | **Funding / exit transactions** | 🌐 public | wallet + token + amount (by design — see below) |
    | **Which addresses ordered** | 🌐 public | orderer addresses are linkable (see limits) |
    
    ## The privacy design decisions
    
    These are the deliberate choices, and the reasoning behind each:
    
    1. **Funding is separate from trading.** The funding transaction necessarily
       reveals wallet, token and amount — that leg is not claimed private. But
       because subsequent orders draw down an accumulated *encrypted* balance, the
       funding amount does not reveal the size or direction of any individual
       order. You can fund once and trade many times.
    
    2. **Direction is encrypted, not just amount.** Most "private swap" designs
       encrypt the amount but leave the direction as a plaintext function argument
       (or worse, log it in an event). That leaks the side you're on even when the
       size is hidden. Veil encrypts direction as an `euint8` and computes the
       per-asset debit with `FHE.select` on the encrypted direction bit — the
       contract never learns which token you are selling.
    
    3. **One shared order path.** Encrypting direction is not enough if selling
       A means calling a different function or a different token wrapper than
       selling B — the *call itself* reveals the side. Both confidential tokens
       are held in the batch contract's shared vault, and both directions go
       through the identical `submitOrder` entry point.
    
    4. **Totals stay encrypted.** A common shortcut is to decrypt the two
       directional totals at close time and compute matching in the clear. That
       hands observers the aggregate order book every round. Veil runs the netting
       *inside FHE* and publicly decrypts only the net residual and surplus side —
       the minimum needed to execute the Uniswap leg.
    
    5. **Payouts stay confidential.** Paying out plain ERC-20 tokens in the
       settlement flow would re-expose the exact amount each user received, and
       link it to their wallet. Payouts are credited to the encrypted vault
       balance instead. Converting to plain tokens is a separate, explicit,
       intentionally-public exit step the user controls.
    
    6. **No encrypted division — by design, not accident.** fhEVM has no
       encrypted÷encrypted division. Rather than leaking totals to compute pro-rata
       shares in the clear, the matching algorithm is structured so division is
       only ever by *public* values (the net residual). Deficit-side payouts are
       exactly their matched amount; the surplus side's Uniswap output share is
       `residual × swapOutput / net`.
    
    ## Architecture
    
    ```
    ┌────────────────────────── Browser (batch.html) ──────────────────────────┐
    │  Minimal UI: balances (plain + 🔒 vault), direction toggle, amount,      │
    │  one "Private swap" button, withdraw buttons, status line, engine log    │
    │                                                                          │
    │  • encrypts order (amount + direction) client-side via relayer SDK       │
    │  • signs txs via raw EIP-1193 (MetaMask)                                 │
    │  • decrypts own vault balances via EIP-712-signed userDecrypt             │
    └───────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                    │ https (localhost:4173)
    ┌───────────────▼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  server.mjs — static host + JSON-RPC proxy (/rpc) + config/artifacts     │
    └───────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                    │ RPC
    ┌───────────────▼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  Ethereum Sepolia                                                        │
    │                                                                          │
    │  VeilBatchSwap (v3)  ← the whole protocol: vault + orders + netting +    │
    │        │                        settlement + confidential payouts        │
    │        │ holds c-vUSD + c-vETH (shared vault)                           │
    │        ▼                                                                 │
    │  ERC7984ERC20Wrapper ×2 (OpenZeppelin) — c-vUSD / c-vETH                 │
    │        │ wrap/unwrap underlying vUSD / vETH (ERC-20 mocks)               │
    │        ▼                                                                 │
    │  Zama FHEVM: ACL, KMS verifier, gateway coprocessors (publicDecrypt,     │
    │  input verification, EIP-712 user decryption)                            │
    │                                                                          │
    │  Uniswap v4 PoolManager + Universal Router + Permit2 — the net leg       │
    └───────────────▲──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                    │
    ┌───────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  keeper.mjs — "the party that's always online"                           │
    │                                                                          │
    │  every 8s: close round → gateway-decrypt (net, surplus) → prepareSettle  │
    │  → executeSettle → creditOrder for every orderer → finalize pending      │
    │  unwraps → liquidity bot (see below)                                     │
    │                                                                          │
    │  Every step it drives is permissionless on-chain — the keeper only pays  │
    │  gas; it holds no privileged power over user funds.                      │
    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    ```
    
    **The liquidity bot.** A round needs at least 2 distinct orderers to close.
    If a single trader has waited out the round window, the keeper submits an
    encrypted counter-order (1 c-unit) from its own funded balance — in a random
    direction, because *directions are encrypted and even the keeper can't see
    which side needs liquidity*. Either way the round completes for the waiting
    trader: opposite direction → matched 1:1 internally; same direction → both
    join the net leg on Uniswap.
    
    ## Live deployment (Sepolia)
    
    Current stack (deployed 2026-11-06, block 12076649):
    
    | Contract | Address |
    |---|---|
    | **VeilBatchSwap v3** | `0x136e2A0F4a48d5f5cFC3821934B86a1B0dA286c0` |
    | c-vUSD (ERC-7984 wrapper of vUSD) | `0x361a17402c7bdc5Ac779e1DB9c1c81395472280a` |
    | c-vETH (ERC-7984 wrapper of vETH) | `0x8F382Fb7221F655dc57796Ca95DF7586b576eaa1` |
    | vUSD (ERC-20 mock, 18 dp, public faucet) | `0x430dCF0916f8a70C9368FAf9CE98Fa0f2Cd7E61D` |
    | vETH (ERC-20 mock, 18 dp, public faucet) | `0xbb10CF47311deC8c9C4085A2Bf637571882095fc` |
    | Uniswap v4 pool (vUSD/vETH, 0.30%, tick 60) | poolId `0xb4dd10273ec0c23ccd4375c06c741452cf8cb86d3c9dc868a161609fa89c7934` |
    
    Round parameters: **60s minimum round duration, ≥2 distinct orderers** to close.
    The full machine-readable record lives in `batch-config.json`.
    
    ## Using the app
    
    1. `npm install && npm run build`, then `SEPOLIA_RPC_URL=… node server.mjs`
    2. Open `http://127.0.0.1:4173/batch.html` in a browser with MetaMask on Sepolia.
    3. **Connect** — the page shows your plain vUSD/vETH and your 🔒 vault balances.
    4. Pick a direction, type an amount, click **Private swap**:
       - if your vault balance is short, the button first funds it (approve → wrap
         → confidential transfer — up to 3 signatures, one time),
       - then it submits your **encrypted order** (one signature).
    5. That's it. The keeper closes the round, nets it, swaps the net on Uniswap,
       and credits your payout **back into your 🔒 vault balance** automatically.
    6. **Withdraw vUSD / vETH** moves vault funds back to plain tokens in your
       wallet (a public leg, finalized by the keeper a minute or two later).
    
    ## The keeper daemon
    
    ```bash
    cd protocol
    export $(grep -v '^#' .env | xargs)          # keeper burner key
    SEPOLIA_RPC_URL=… node scripts/keeper.mjs    # 8s tick loop
    ```
    
    Responsibilities, in tick order: close eligible rounds → gateway-decrypt the
    net/surplus (with retries — gateway propagation is asynchronous) →
    `prepareSettle` → `executeSettle` → `creditOrder` for every orderer found in
    `OrderSubmitted` events → finalize any pending unwrap requests → liquidity bot.
    All of these are permissionless contract calls; the keeper is an automation,
    not an authority. It also funds its own vault once (tracked in
    `scripts/bot-funded.json`) so its counter-orders draw on encrypted balances.
    
    ## Contract API reference
    
    `VeilBatchSwap` (v3) — the complete protocol in one contract:
    
    | Function | Who | What |
    |---|---|---|
    | `onConfidentialTransferReceived` | wrappers | funding: empty/`0x00` data credits the caller's encrypted vault balance |
    | `fundedBalance(user, asset)` | view | encrypted handle of a vault balance (decrypt it yourself) |
    | `submitOrder(encAmt, proof, encDir, proof)` | anyone | encrypted order from the vault balance; one order per user per round; max 6 per round |
    | `closeRound()` | anyone | after the timer + threshold: encrypted pairwise netting; only net + surplus become publicly decryptable |
    | `prepareSettle(roundId, clearValues, proof)` | settler | verifies gateway signatures over (net, surplus); sets the on-chain 90% slippage floor; opens the unwrap request |
    | `executeSettle(roundId, cleartext, proof)` | anyone | finalizes the unwrap, swaps the net on Uniswap v4, wraps the output, enforces the floor |
    | `creditOrder(roundId, user)` | anyone | credits the orderer's payout into their encrypted vault balance; single-credit enforced |
    | `exit(asset, encAmt, proof)` | anyone | unwrap part of your own vault balance to plain tokens (public leg) |
    
    Events: `Funded(user, asset)`, `OrderSubmitted(round, user)` — **no direction,
    no amount** — `RoundClosed`, `RoundPrepared(round, net, unwrapHandle)`,
    `RoundSettled(round, surplusSide, net, swapOutput)` — **no matched amount** —
    `OrderCredited(round, user)`.
    
    ## Running it yourself
    
    ```bash
    # root: web app
    npm install
    npm test          # unit + server tests
    npm run build     # vite build → dist/
    
    # protocol: contracts
    cd protocol
    npm install
    npx hardhat compile
    npx hardhat test                    # FHEVM mock network, 9 tests
    
    # deploy a fresh stack to Sepolia (uses protocol/.env burner keys)
    VEIL_REDEPLOY_BATCH=1 SEPOLIA_RPC_URL=… node scripts/deploy-stack.mjs
    # optional knobs: VEIL_MIN_ROUND_SECONDS=30 VEIL_MIN_USERS=2
    
    # seed the demo pool with two-sided liquidity, then run the keeper
    node scripts/seed-pool.mjs
    SEPOLIA_RPC_URL=… node scripts/keeper.mjs
    ```
    
    `batch-config.json` is the single source of truth for deployed addresses; the
    server exposes it at `/batch-config` and the UI refuses to run against a
    contract whose `VERSION()` doesn't match.
    
    ## Testing
    
    - **`protocol/test/veil-batch-swap.test.ts`** (9 tests, FHEVM mock network):
      funding credits encrypted balances; order events carry no direction/amount;
      encrypted direction debits the correct side; underfunded orders are silently
      zeroed; two-sided rounds settle with correct matched/surplus math; fully
      matched rounds need no swap leg; creditOrder is permissionless but
      single-credit; exit unwraps to plain tokens; one order per user per round.
    - **Root tests** (`npm test`): server proxy, fee-sizing, config handling.
    - **Live E2E** (Sepolia, proven 2026-11-06): fund → encrypted order → bot
      counter-order → encrypted close → `net=2000000, surplusSide=A` decrypt →
      Uniswap swap → confidential credit (vault grew by 5.78 c-vETH) →
      `exit` → 5.0 plain vETH received.
    
    ## Honest privacy limits
    
    This demo is built to be honest about what it does *not* hide:
    
    - **Funding and exits are public.** The wallet, token and amount of the
      fund/unwrap legs are visible. The design claim is narrower: those amounts do
      not reveal individual order sizes or directions.
    - **Orderer addresses are linkable.** `OrderSubmitted` and `OrderCredited`
      carry the user's address (required for permissionless crediting). An
      observer learns *that* an address traded in a round — not which side, not
      how much, not what it received. On-chain storage of the orderer array is
      also readable by anyone with `eth_getStorageAt`.
    - **The net trade is public.** The residual must trade on Uniswap; its size,
      direction and timing are visible. Batching makes the *net* much smaller than
      the sum of individual orders, which blurs order patterns — it is **not** an
      anonymity guarantee, and with few participants the net can approximate a
      single order.
    - **Round metadata is public.** Round timing, the token pair, and the fact
      that you interacted with the protocol at all.
    - **FHE trust model.** Confidentiality rests on the Zama FHEVM threshold
      cryptography (KMS/gateway coprocessors), not on this contract alone.
    - **The demo pair is 1:1.** Internal matching assumes parity pricing. Real
      pairs need price-aware matching, per-order execution bounds, and an
      independent oracle before any of this is production-viable.
    
    ## Security and trust assumptions
    
    - **The settler role.** Only the immutable configured settler (the keeper's
      address) may call `prepareSettle` and commit the gateway-verified values;
      `executeSettle` is permissionless and cannot override the on-chain slippage
      floor. The settler cannot steal funds — it can only delay settlement or
      commit wrong decryptions, which the gateway signature verification rejects.
    - **Slippage guard.** `minSwapOutput = 90% × net` is computed on-chain at
      prepare time; execution reverts if the Uniswap leg returns less. For the
      seeded ~1:1 demo pool this is a conservative bound, not an oracle price.
    - **Underfunded orders.** An order larger than the encrypted vault balance is
      silently zeroed (the OpenZeppelin confidential-token pattern): no revert
      oracle leaks balance information, and the UI checks your own decryptable
      balance before submitting.
    - **Burner keys.** The keeper/operator keys in `protocol/.env` are testnet
      burners, gitignored, and hold no authority over user funds.
    - **Not audited.** Zero external review. Do not deploy this with real value.
    
    ## Repository layout
    
    ```
    ├── batch.html                  # the minimal private-swap UI
    ├── src/batch.js                # UI logic (encrypt, sign, decrypt, fund)
    ├── server.mjs                  # static host + /rpc proxy + /batch-config
    ├── batch-config.json           # live deployment record (addresses, knobs)
    ├── index.html, src/app.js      # legacy RAILGUN wallet demo (separate app)
    ├── test/                       # root unit tests
    └── protocol/
        ├── contracts/VeilBatchSwap.sol        # the whole protocol (v3)
        ├── contracts/VeilConfidentialWrapper.sol
        ├── contracts/mocks/                   # ERC20Mock, MockBatchSwap (1:1 test double)
        ├── test/veil-batch-swap.test.ts       # 9-test FHEVM mock suite
        ├── scripts/
        │   ├── deploy-stack.mjs    # deploy/redeploy the stack, write batch-config.json
        │   ├── seed-pool.mjs       # two-sided liquidity for the demo pool
        │   ├── keeper.mjs          # the automation daemon (close→settle→credit→bot)
        │   ├── verify-v3.mjs       # live E2E: fund + encrypted order + self-decrypt
        │   ├── check-vault.mjs     # decrypt your own vault balances on live
        │   └── test-exit.mjs       # live E2E: exit to plain tokens
        └── .env                    # burner keys (gitignored)
    ```
    
    ## Known limitations and future work
    
    - **Order cap per round** (6) bounds the O(n²) encrypted-netting gas; larger
      batches need a cheaper netting strategy.
    - **One order per user per round** — multiple orders from one address in the
      same round are rejected (they would self-net; supporting them needs
      per-order accounting).
    - **1:1 internal pricing** — real pairs need price-aware matching.
    - **No MEV protection on the net leg** — the residual trades with a 90% floor;
      a production version would use on-chain oracle bounds or batch auctions.
    - **Session keys (ERC-7710)** would remove the repeated signatures for
      funding/wrapping.
    - **Address-linkability** of orderers is the biggest remaining privacy gap;
      designs like stealth-address registration or a separate commitment layer
      (e.g. a nullifier tree) are the natural next step.
    
    ---
    
    *Built for ETHGlobal with Zama FHEVM, OpenZeppelin confidential contracts, and
    Uniswap v4. Testnet only — everything here can and will change.*
    # Prawl
    
    A privacy-first Ethereum wallet for the browser.
    
    - **Private balance first.** Your main account is a RAILGUN shielded balance; the public address is only the way in and out.
    - **No IP or address linking at the RPC layer.** Every chain read and write goes over Tor via Anon-RPC, with a separate circuit per identity: the wallet's account, each transaction, each one-time sender, and the shared RAILGUN scan.
    - **No seed phrase, no server.** Keys come from a passkey (WebAuthn PRF). The same synced passkey on another device restores the wallet.
    
    ## What it does
    
    | Step | How |
    |---|---|
    | Create | A passkey derives the RAILGUN spending/viewing keys, the public account key and a storage key (HKDF). A new wallet opens from our public pool snapshot in about 20 s over Tor. |
    | Fund | Send Sepolia ETH to the public account, then **Shield ETH** into the private balance. |
    | Settle | New shields wait for the PPOI check (2–4 minutes measured on Sepolia; up to an hour on mainnet). The wallet refreshes every minute and shows each note's PPOI state. |
    | Pay privately | Private send to a `0zk` address as an ERC-4337 UserOperation from a fresh one-time EIP-7702 sender. The RAILGUN paymaster takes gas from the private balance, so the sender has no ETH and no history. Queued PPOI proofs are submitted on the following syncs. |
    | Exit | Withdraw to a public address as native ETH (or WETH), with warnings when that would link you (own account, right after a shield). Gas comes from the private balance; the wallet checks it fits before proving. |
    | Offline cash | The Prawl Android app pays phone to phone with no network (hardware one-time keys). Here: **load** a phone-minted coin, from the public account or **privately** (commit, RAILGUN unshield to the coin's deposit address, activate); **cash out** to a public address or **into the private balance** (a shield target the phone signs over); **refresh** a coin before it expires. Links and QR codes (`#/offline/{load,load-private,redeem,redeem-shield,refresh}?d=…`), escrow v3 and v3.1 on Sepolia; everything is simulated before sending and judged paid only from the escrow's events. |
    | Activity | A local history of shields, sends, withdrawals and offline loads, encrypted on the device with the passkey-derived key. Hashes copy; there are no explorer links, which would reveal your IP and which transaction is yours. |
    | Restore | On a new device, the passkey returns the wallet's creation time; the scan starts from the snapshot and replays only from then. **Full rescan** is the escape hatch. |
    
    The page has four tabs (Wallet, Offline cash, Activity, Network); a link from the phone opens the Offline tab. Nothing secret touches disk: RAILGUN checkpoints are sealed with AES-GCM under the passkey-derived storage key.
    
    ## Layout
    
    | Path | What |
    |---|---|
    | `packages/anon-rpc` | Anon-RPC client: Tor worker host, per-identity isolation, deadlines and retries, batching, a `fetch` for RAILGUN |
    | `packages/keys` | Passkey PRF root, key derivation, sealed storage |
    | `packages/railgun` | Wrapper around kohaku-railgun-wasm: queued session, windowed sync, snapshots, transport over Anon-RPC |
    | `apps/wallet` | The wallet web app (RAILGUN runs in a worker whose `fetch` is bridged to Anon-RPC) |
    | `gateway/` | Build, start and stop our own tor-js gateway on this machine |
    | `vendor/torjs-worker` | Patch and build script for the keep-alive tor-js worker |
    | `scripts/` | Live smoke tests in Chromium with a virtual passkey |
    
    ## Run
    
    ```sh
    pnpm install
    gateway/build.sh                              # once: tor-js gateway into .local/bin
    vendor/torjs-worker/build.sh                  # once: patched worker into .local/worker
    packages/railgun/scripts/build-wasm.sh        # once: kohaku-railgun-wasm (+ our patches) into .local/railgun
    node packages/railgun/scripts/build-snapshot.mjs   # public pool snapshot; rerun to refresh (resumes)
    gateway/start.sh                              # our own gateway, plus a fresh Tor snapshot
    pnpm dev                                      # http://localhost:5173 (passkeys need "localhost", not an IP)
    gateway/stop.sh                               # when done
    ```
    
    Without a local gateway the wallet falls back to the public demo gateway.
    
    ## Deploy (static site)
    
    ```sh
    pnpm --filter ./apps/wallet build:web   # apps/wallet/dist-web: app + worker, Tor bootstrap, wasm, snapshot, vercel.json
    ```
    
    Deploy `apps/wallet/dist-web` to any static host (Vercel: `vercel deploy apps/wallet/dist-web`). The build uses the gateway in `VITE_PRAWL_GATEWAY` (default: the public demo gateway). A visitor can switch with a `?gateway=<ip:port:certhash>` link, which asks them to confirm. Passkeys are bound to the site's domain: choose the final domain before real wallets are made. Refresh the Tor bootstrap and pool snapshot with each deploy (the bootstrap goes stale in about 3 days).
    
    ## Test
    
    ```sh
    pnpm test
    CHROME=/path/to/chromium node scripts/smoke.mjs                       # boots Tor in Chromium and reads over it
    CHROME=/path/to/chromium RESTORE=1 node scripts/smoke-wallet.mjs URL  # create, wipe, restore a wallet over Tor
    CHROME=/path/to/chromium node scripts/live-funded.mjs URL DIR         # live Sepolia: shield, PPOI, private send, withdraw
    CHROME=/path/to/chromium node scripts/live-offline.mjs URL DIR        # live Sepolia: offline-cash load and cash-out links
    ```
    ✓ • 0ms

## Assistant

Yes. The intended result is a **private swap feature inside the existing Prawl wallet**, using the ETHGlobal prototype’s encrypted batching mechanism, with one Uniswap pool between the wallet’s token and a new custom ERC-20. The existing wallet flows should continue to work.

I’ve inspected the main architecture and implementation entry points in both projects. One qualification: I have not yet completed a line-by-line review of every file in `ethglobal`, so the plan below identifies the areas that need deeper verification before implementation rather than treating the prototype’s README claims as proven.

The central integration challenge is that Prawl’s private balance is **RAILGUN**, while Veil’s swap balances are in a **separate FHE-encrypted vault**. They cannot be treated as the same balance. Moving between them requires an explicit bridge flow, and the prototype does not make every part of that flow private.

## What the prototype currently does

The relevant implementation is in [VeilBatchSwap.sol](/home/sahilkaushik/study_material/blockchain/prawl/ethglobal/protocol/contracts/VeilBatchSwap.sol), its [confidential wrapper](/home/sahilkaushik/study_material/blockchain/prawl/ethglobal/protocol/contracts/VeilConfidentialWrapper.sol), [batch.js](/home/sahilkaushik/study_material/blockchain/prawl/ethglobal/src/batch.js), and the [keeper](/home/sahilkaushik/study_material/blockchain/prawl/ethglobal/protocol/scripts/keeper.mjs).

Its flow is:

1. Deposit ordinary ERC-20 tokens into confidential wrappers and fund the swap vault.
2. Encrypt the order amount and direction in the browser and submit both through the same contract function.
3. After the round closes, match opposite orders under FHE.
4. Reveal the aggregate unmatched amount and direction, then swap that residual through Uniswap v4.
5. Credit each user’s result to their encrypted swap-vault balance.
6. Optionally exit the vault back to ordinary tokens.

The prototype uses **two mock tokens**. Prawl currently uses Sepolia ETH, with **WETH inside RAILGUN**, so the proposed pair is **Sepolia WETH / a new custom ERC-20**. That requires a new deployment and pool; the current prototype pool cannot simply be reused because its assets are different.

## Privacy boundary to preserve

The encrypted order amount, direction, and vault payout are the privacy properties to carry over. The following remain visible in the prototype:

- The address submitting an order and the round in which it participated.
- Vault funding and exit transactions, including the underlying token and amount.
- The pair, round timing, and the net residual traded on Uniswap.
- Potential inferences when only a few people participate.

The keeper’s small counter-order helps a round complete, but does **not** provide meaningful anonymity by itself. The UI and documentation must describe this accurately; it would be misleading to promise that everything about a swap is private.

## Detailed implementation plan

### 1. Finish the end-to-end technical review

Before changing code, trace the actual contract, browser, deployment scripts, keeper and tests together:

- Verify encrypted balance accounting, wrapper conversion, rounding, overflow bounds and payout calculations.
- Trace each state transition: funding, ordering, closing, decryption, settlement, crediting and exit.
- Check contract permissions and recovery paths, especially failed settlement, stalled decryption and unavailable keeper.
- Verify the exact SDK APIs, chain configuration, contract version checks, and browser requirements.
- Map Prawl’s signing, transaction submission, Tor transport, passkey-derived keys, RAILGUN worker and activity storage to the operations the swap needs.

Deliverable: a confirmed integration design with any prototype defects and required contract changes identified before deployment.

### 2. Define the one-pool testnet setup

Use **Sepolia WETH / a custom test ERC-20** as the initial pair, with one Uniswap v4 pool.

- Create a simple, clearly labelled test ERC-20 with defined decimals and a testnet distribution method.
- Deploy confidential wrappers for WETH and the custom token.
- Deploy a batch contract configured for those wrappers and the new pool.
- Initialize and seed the pool with test liquidity.
- Record chain ID, token addresses, wrapper addresses, pool key, router, Permit2, batch contract version, deployment block and keeper configuration in one validated deployment manifest.
- Fail clearly if the wallet is connected to the wrong chain, contracts or pool.

**Pricing constraint:** the prototype matches opposite orders at **1:1** and applies a floor based on that assumption. WETH and an arbitrary custom token do not naturally have equal value. For the first demo, the custom token and pool must be deliberately configured and maintained as a test-only parity pair, with explicit limitations. A real-valued pair needs price-aware matching, execution bounds and an oracle or equivalent pricing design; it cannot safely inherit the prototype’s fixed 1:1 accounting.

### 3. Integrate the swap protocol without replacing wallet infrastructure

Add a dedicated swap module to the wallet rather than importing the prototype’s standalone page wholesale.

- Port the required contract ABIs, encryption, decryption and transaction-building logic from `batch.js` into typed wallet code.
- Adapt signing to Prawl’s own account and key lifecycle instead of requiring MetaMask’s `window.ethereum`.
- Route chain reads and writes through the existing Anon-RPC/Tor infrastructure where supported.
- Investigate the FHE SDK’s gateway requests separately: ensure they do not silently bypass Prawl’s network privacy model, and document any unavoidable metadata exposure.
- Keep FHE keys, decryption authorizations and transaction state scoped to the unlocked wallet; avoid storing secrets in plaintext.
- Integrate as a new wallet feature while preserving existing RAILGUN, offline cash, settings, network and activity flows.

The exact signer adapter and network transport are feasibility checks, not assumptions: the prototype uses MetaMask and its own RPC proxy, while Prawl has a different architecture.

### 4. Design the RAILGUN-to-swap funding path

Offer a clear way to fund the swap vault from the wallet’s WETH balance.

- Assess whether Prawl’s existing RAILGUN unshield and subsequent-call support can safely deliver WETH to an account that can wrap and fund the FHE vault.
- Verify who becomes the vault owner, who signs encrypted inputs, how gas is paid, and whether the selected account can later decrypt and exit its balance.
- Trace what is visible on chain and whether funding links a RAILGUN unshield to the swap identity.
- If an atomic or one-time-account flow is not supported, implement a clear staged funding flow using the wallet’s public account, explicitly showing the public amount and linkability.
- Account for RAILGUN fees, gas, token approvals, wrapper precision and any required public ETH.

This is the most important integration decision: an apparently seamless button must not obscure where funds sit or claim that the transfer between the two privacy systems is hidden.

### 5. Implement the swap and balance experience

Add a **Swap** view to the wallet with:

- The single WETH/custom-token pair and direction selector.
- Separately labelled RAILGUN balance, public balance where relevant, and encrypted swap-vault balances.
- Amount entry, estimated fees and the applicable test-pool pricing limitation.
- Explicit funding and exit steps, including their public visibility.
- Client-side encryption of amount and direction, followed by order submission.
- Round and order progress: submitted, waiting, closing, settling, credited, or action required.
- Refresh and recovery after reload or interruption, using contract state rather than assuming a submitted transaction succeeded.
- Decryption of the user’s own vault balances, with errors surfaced clearly.

The prototype permits one order per address per round and caps a round at six orders. The UI must handle both restrictions and show when a round is waiting for enough participants.

### 6. Handle output and return to Prawl

A swap result initially belongs to the **FHE vault**, not automatically to the RAILGUN balance.

- Provide an explicit vault exit to ordinary WETH or custom tokens.
- Investigate and implement re-shielding to RAILGUN where the chain, token and existing wallet support it.
- Extend token balance display and token-specific shield/unshield handling where needed for the custom ERC-20.
- Show fees and the public exit/re-shield boundary before the user proceeds.
- Retain recoverable state if an exit is asynchronous or the keeper stops before finalization.

If the custom token cannot be supported by the existing RAILGUN integration as deployed, the initial feature must say so and keep its output in the swap vault or public account until that support is implemented.

### 7. Operate and harden settlement

Adapt the prototype keeper to the new deployment:

- Automate round closure, gateway decryption, settlement, order crediting and pending exits.
- Make retries idempotent and persist enough state to recover after restart.
- Surface stalled rounds, insufficient gas, failed decryptions, liquidity shortages and failed swaps.
- Review the immutable settler’s actual authority and liveness implications.
- Treat the counter-order bot as a testnet liveness aid, with separate funded test assets and no claim of anonymity.
- Define a practical recovery procedure for each stage before funds are used.

### 8. Test and verify the complete flow

Testing should cover both the new feature and preservation of the existing wallet:

- **Contract tests:** both directions, matching and residual swaps, rounding and wrapper units, insufficient encrypted balance, duplicate orders, full rounds, zero net, slippage failure, repeated credit, exit and failure recovery.
- **Integration tests:** signer and FHE SDK compatibility, Tor routing, configuration validation, transaction lifecycle and reload recovery.
- **Wallet regression tests:** existing passkey, sync, shield, private send, withdrawal, offline cash and activity behavior.
- **Sepolia end-to-end:** deploy and seed the pool; fund the vault; submit orders from multiple independent accounts in both directions; settle against Uniswap; decrypt credited balances; exit and re-shield supported assets.
- **Privacy review:** inspect transactions, logs, RPC/gateway traffic and small-batch inference against the claims presented in the UI.

Completion means a user can move supported funds into the swap vault, place an encrypted order, receive the confidential credit and recover or exit it, while the wallet’s existing tests and core flows still pass.

## Decisions to confirm before implementation

My proposed initial assumptions are **Sepolia**, **WETH/custom ERC-20**, a deliberately **1:1 test pair**, and the prototype’s existing FHE batching model. The main design choice still requiring verification is whether the RAILGUN-to-FHE transfer can be made safely with Prawl’s current one-time-account transaction flow. If it cannot, the first version should use an explicit public funding step rather than imply a private bridge.

No code or deployment changes have been made.

        "build:web": "tsc -p tsconfig.json && VITE_PRAWL_GATEWAY=${VITE_PRAWL_GATEWAY:-152.236.3.22:12298:uEiCH0VdbhryzJcMtCfB0vLA4sOvHdQ4hq5w7CopcwU59bQ} vite build --outDir dist-web && node scripts/export-static.mjs dist-web"
      },
      "dependencies": {
        "@anon-rpc/browser-harness": "0.4.0",
        "@prawl/anon-rpc": "workspace:*",
        "@prawl/keys": "workspace:*",
        "@prawl/railgun": "workspace:*",
        "jsqr": "1.4.0",
        "qrcode": "1.5.4",
        "react": "^19.3.1",
        "react-dom": "^19.3.1",
        "viem": "2.57.14"
      },
      "devDependencies": {
        "@tailwindcss/vite": "^4.3.4",
        "@types/node": "^26.6.6",
        "@types/qrcode": "1.5.5",
        "@types/react": "^19.3.0",
        "@types/react-dom": "^19.3.0",
        "@vitejs/plugin-react": "^6.2.1",
        "tailwindcss": "^4.3.4",
        "typescript": "5.9.3",
        "vite": "^8.3.6",
        "vitest": "5.0.5"
      }
    }
    /**
     * Veil — minimal confidential swap UI.
     *
     * One screen: balances, an encrypted order (direction + amount), done.
     * Funding/wrap happens automatically inside the swap button when the vault
     * balance is short; payouts are credited back to the vault by the keeper;
     * "Withdraw" exits to plain tokens (a public leg by design).
     */
    
    import { BrowserProvider, Contract, Interface, JsonRpcProvider, formatUnits, getAddress, parseUnits, hexlify } from 'ethers';
    
    const SEPOLIA_CHAIN_ID = 11155111n;
    const RELAYER_SDK_URL = 'https://cdn.zama.org/relayer-sdk-js/0.4.0/relayer-sdk-js.umd.cjs';
    const RELAYER_SDK_INTEGRITY = 'sha384-d7nXd4gIttpUf2+vxutKvSBeTFtLklJGWBRO0X2f5s7gW1kHOETmSaRfFvZ7GWEd';
    
    const $ = (id) => document.getElementById(id);
    const el = (id) => {
      const node = $(id);
      if (!node) throw new Error(`Missing #${id}`);
      return node;
    };
    
    const engineLog = [];
    function appendEngineLog(kind, message) {
      engineLog.unshift({ at: new Date().toISOString(), kind, message });
      if (engineLog.length > 120) engineLog.pop();
      try { localStorage.setItem('veil.batch.engineLog.v2', JSON.stringify(engineLog)); } catch {}
      const list = el('engineLogList');
      list.innerHTML = '';
      for (const entry of engineLog.slice(0, 60)) {
        const row = document.createElement('div');
        row.className = 'engine-log-line';
        const time = document.createElement('span');
        time.className = 'engine-log-time';
        time.textContent = entry.at.slice(11, 19);
        const text = document.createElement('span');
        text.textContent = `${entry.kind}: ${entry.message}`;
        row.append(time, text);
        list.append(row);
      }
    }
    
    // ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    // State, providers, wallet
    // ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    const state = {
      account: null,
      browserProvider: null,
      readProvider: null,
      fhevm: null,
      config: null,
      direction: 1, // 1 = vUSD→vETH, 2 = vETH→vUSD
      funded: [null, null], // decrypted vault balances in c-units
      pollTimer: null,
    };
    
    function readProvider() {
      if (!state.readProvider) {
        state.readProvider = new JsonRpcProvider(new URL('/rpc', window.location.href).href, SEPOLIA_CHAIN_ID, { staticNetwork: true });
      }
      return state.readProvider;
    }
    
    function writeProvider() {
      if (!state.account) throw new Error('Connect wallet first.');
      if (!state.browserProvider) state.browserProvider = new BrowserProvider(window.ethereum, 'any');
      return state.browserProvider;
    }
    
    function requireConfig() {
      if (!state.config) throw new Error('Deployed stack not loaded yet.');
      return state.config;
    }
    
    function batchContract(provider) {
      const config = requireConfig();
      return new Contract(config.batch, new Interface([
        'function VERSION() view returns (uint256)',
        'function currentRound() view returns (uint256)',
        'function lastUpdateTime() view returns (uint256)',
        'function fundedBalance(address,uint8) view returns (bytes32)',
        'function submitOrder(bytes32,bytes,bytes32,bytes)',
        'function exit(uint8,bytes32,bytes)',
        'event OrderSubmitted(uint256 indexed round, address indexed user)',
      ]), provider);
    }
    
    function underlyingContract(which, provider) {
      const config = requireConfig();
      return new Contract(which === 'A' ? config.tokenA : config.tokenB, new Interface([
        'function balanceOf(address) view returns (uint256)',
        'function allowance(address,address) view returns (uint256)',
        'function approve(address,uint256) returns (bool)',
      ]), provider);
    }
    
    function wrapperContract(which, provider) {
      const config = requireConfig();
      return new Contract(which === 'A' ? config.cTokenA : config.cTokenB, new Interface([
        'function wrap(address,uint256)',
        'function rate() view returns (uint256)',
        'function confidentialTransferAndCall(address,bytes32,bytes,bytes)',
      ]), provider);
    }
    
    function shorten(address) {
      return address ? `${address.slice(0, 6)}…${address.slice(-4)}` : '—';
    }
    
    function setStatus(text, kind = '') {
      const node = el('status');
      node.textContent = text;
      node.className = `status ${kind}`;
    }
    
    async function connectWallet() {
      if (!window.ethereum) { setStatus('MetaMask not detected.', 'err'); return; }
      const accounts = await window.ethereum.request({ method: 'eth_requestAccounts' });
      state.account = getAddress(accounts[0]); // EIP-55 — the FHEVM SDK requires it
      state.browserProvider = new BrowserProvider(window.ethereum, 'any');
      const network = await state.browserProvider.getNetwork();
      if (network.chainId !== SEPOLIA_CHAIN_ID) { setStatus('Wrong network — switch to Sepolia.', 'err'); return; }
      el('connectBtn').textContent = shorten(state.account);
      el('connectBtn').classList.add('ok');
      el('swapBtn').disabled = false;
      appendEngineLog('wallet', `connected ${state.account}`);
      await getFhevm();
      await refreshAll();
      state.pollTimer = state.pollTimer || setInterval(() => refreshAll().catch(() => {}), 10000);
    }
    
    window.ethereum?.on?.('accountsChanged', (accounts) => {
      state.account = accounts[0] ? getAddress(accounts[0]) : null;
      state.browserProvider = null;
      state.funded = [null, null];
      el('connectBtn').textContent = state.account ? shorten(state.account) : 'Connect';
      el('connectBtn').classList.toggle('ok', !!state.account);
      if (state.account) refreshAll().catch(() => {});
    });
    
    // ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    // Tx helpers (raw EIP-1193; retries ride out async FHE input-proof checks)
    // ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    async function sendTx(label, txRequest) {
      const from = state.account;
      appendEngineLog('tx', `${label}: requesting signature`);
      const hash = await window.ethereum.request({ method: 'eth_sendTransaction', params: [{ from, ...txRequest }] });
      appendEngineLog('tx', `${label}: broadcast ${hash}`);
      const receipt = await readProvider().waitForTransaction(hash);
      if (receipt.status !== 1) throw new Error(`${label} reverted: ${hash}`);
      appendEngineLog('tx', `${label}: mined in block ${receipt.blockNumber}`);
      return receipt;
    }
    
    async function sendTxWithInputRetry(label, txRequest, attempts = 6) {
      let lastError;
      for (let i = 0; i < attempts; i++) {
        try {
          return await sendTx(label, txRequest);
        } catch (error) {
          lastError = error;
          if (!/reverted/i.test(String(error.message))) throw error;
          appendEngineLog('tx', `${label}: reverted — FHE input proof not yet verified; retry ${i + 1}/${attempts - 1} in 12s`);
          setStatus(`Retrying ${label} (FHE input verification pending)…`);
          await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 12000));
        }
      }
      throw lastError;
    }
    
    // ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    // FHEVM instance (relayer SDK from CDN) + user decryption
    // ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    let sdkReady = null;
    
    function loadRelayerSdk() {
      if (window.relayerSDK) return Promise.resolve();
      return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
        const script = document.createElement('script');
        script.src = RELAYER_SDK_URL;
        script.async = true;
        script.integrity = RELAYER_SDK_INTEGRITY;
        script.crossOrigin = 'anonymous';
        script.addEventListener('load', () => resolve());
        script.addEventListener('error', () => reject(new Error('Relayer SDK failed to load')));
        document.head.append(script);
      });
    }
    
    async function getFhevm() {
      if (state.fhevm) return state.fhevm;
      if (!sdkReady) {
        sdkReady = (async () => {
          appendEngineLog('sdk', 'loading Zama relayer SDK…');
          await loadRelayerSdk();
          const ok = await window.relayerSDK.initSDK();
          if (!ok) throw new Error('initSDK failed');
          return true;
        })();
      }
      await sdkReady;
      const sdk = window.relayerSDK;
      const instance = await sdk.createInstance({
        ...sdk.SepoliaConfig,
        network: window.ethereum ?? new URL('/rpc', window.location.href).href,
      });
      state.fhevm = instance;
      appendEngineLog('sdk', 'FHEVM instance ready');
      return instance;
    }
    
    let decryptSig = null;
    async function userDecryptEuint(handle, contractAddress) {
      const instance = await getFhevm();
      if (!decryptSig) {
        const signer = await writeProvider().getSigner();
        const keypair = instance.generateKeypair();
        const config = requireConfig();
        const contractAddresses = [config.batch, config.cTokenA, config.cTokenB];
        const startTimestamp = Math.floor(Date.now() / 1000);
        const durationDays = 365;
        const eip712 = instance.createEIP712(keypair.publicKey, contractAddresses, startTimestamp, durationDays);
        const signature = await signer.signTypedData(
          eip712.domain,
          { UserDecryptRequestVerification: eip712.types.UserDecryptRequestVerification },
          eip712.message,
        );
        decryptSig = { privateKey: keypair.privateKey, publicKey: keypair.publicKey, signature, contractAddresses, startTimestamp, durationDays, userAddress: state.account };
        appendEngineLog('sdk', 'user-decrypt EIP-712 signature cached (365d)');
      }
      const results = await instance.userDecrypt(
        [{ handle: hexlify(handle), contractAddress }],
        decryptSig.privateKey, decryptSig.publicKey, decryptSig.signature,
        decryptSig.contractAddresses, decryptSig.userAddress, decryptSig.startTimestamp, decryptSig.durationDays,
      );
      return results[hexlify(handle)];
    }
    
    // ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    // Config + balances
    // ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    async function loadConfig() {
      let config = null;
      try {
        const response = await fetch('/batch-config');
        if (response.ok) config = await response.json();
      } catch {}
      state.config = config?.deployed ? config : null;
      if (state.config) {
        try {
          const version = await (await batchContract(readProvider())).VERSION();
          state.config.compatible = version === 3n;
        } catch { state.config.compatible = false; }
      }
      if (!state.config) setStatus('No deployed stack found — run the deploy script.', 'err');
      else if (!state.config.compatible) setStatus('Deployed contract is an older version — redeploy required.', 'err');
    }
    
    async function refreshAll() {
      if (!state.config) return;
      await readBalances();
      await renderRoundLine();
    }
    
    async function readBalances() {
      const config = requireConfig();
      if (state.account) {
        const signer = await writeProvider().getSigner();
        const a = underlyingContract('A', signer);
        const b = underlyingContract('B', signer);
        el('balA').textContent = `${formatUnits(await a.balanceOf(state.account), 18)} vUSD`;
        el('balB').textContent = `${formatUnits(await b.balanceOf(state.account), 18)} vETH`;
        const batch = batchContract(readProvider());
        for (const [asset, nodeId] of [[0, 'balCA'], [1, 'balCB']]) {
          try {
            const handle = await batch.fundedBalance(state.account, asset);
            const value = handle === 0n ? 0n : await userDecryptEuint(handle, config.batch);
            state.funded[asset] = value;
            el(nodeId).textContent = formatUnits(value, 6);
          } catch (error) {
            appendEngineLog('sdk', `vault balance ${asset}: ${String(error.message).slice(0, 60)}`);
          }
        }
      }
    }
    
    async function renderRoundLine() {
      const batch = batchContract(readProvider());
      const current = Number(await batch.currentRound());
      const round = current - 1;
      if (round < 1) { el('roundLine').textContent = `batch round ${current} open`; return; }
      const r = await (await batchContract(readProvider())).rounds(round);
      const lastUpdate = Number(await batch.lastUpdateTime());
      const duration = Number(state.config.minRoundDuration ?? 60);
      const closesIn = Math.max(0, lastUpdate + duration - Math.floor(Date.now() / 1000));
      const state_ = r.executed ? 'settled — payouts credited' : r.prepared ? 'swapping on Uniswap…' : r.closed ? 'decrypting totals…' : `open — closes in ${closesIn}s`;
      el('roundLine').textContent = `round ${round}: ${state_}`;
    }
    
    // ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    // Private swap — auto-fund if needed, then submit the encrypted order
    // ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    async function privateSwap() {
      const config = requireConfig();
      if (!state.account) { setStatus('Connect wallet first.', 'err'); return; }
      const amount = parseUnits(el('amount').value || '0', 6);
      if (amount <= 0n) { setStatus('Enter an amount.', 'err'); return; }
      el('swapBtn').disabled = true;
      try {
        const which = state.direction === 1 ? 'A' : 'B'; // asset being sold
        const asset = which === 'A' ? 0 : 1;
        const have = state.funded[asset] ?? 0n;
        if (have < amount) {
          setStatus('Funding your confidential balance…');
          await fundVault(which, amount);
        }
        setStatus('Encrypting order (direction + amount)…');
        const instance = await getFhevm();
        const enc = await instance.createEncryptedInput(config.batch, state.account).add64(amount).add8(state.direction).encrypt();
        setStatus('Submitting encrypted order…');
        await sendTxWithInputRetry('encrypted order', {
          to: config.batch,
          data: batchContract(await writeProvider().getSigner()).interface.encodeFunctionData('submitOrder', [enc.handles[0], enc.inputProof, enc.handles[1], enc.inputProof]),
        });
        el('amount').value = '';
        setStatus('Order submitted ✓ — the batch settles automatically and your payout lands in your vault balance.', 'ok');
        appendEngineLog('flow', `order submitted (direction and amount encrypted)`);
        await refreshAll();
      } catch (error) {
        setStatus(String(error.message?.shortMessage || error.message).slice(0, 160), 'err');
        appendEngineLog('error', String(error.message?.shortMessage || error.message).slice(0, 160));
      } finally {
        el('swapBtn').disabled = false;
      }
    }
    
    /** Wrap underlying → c-tokens and move them into the batch vault (public leg). */
    async function fundVault(which, cAmount) {
      const config = requireConfig();
      const signer = await writeProvider().getSigner();
      const wrapper = wrapperContract(which, signer);
      const wrapperAddress = await wrapper.getAddress();
      const underlying = underlyingContract(which, signer);
      const rate = await wrapper.rate();
      const uAmount = cAmount * rate;
      const balance = await underlying.balanceOf(state.account);
      if (balance < uAmount) {
        // testnet faucet — the deployed mocks expose mint to anyone
        setStatus(`Minting testnet ${which === 'A' ? 'vUSD' : 'vETH'}…`);
        await sendTx(`faucet ${which === 'A' ? 'vUSD' : 'vETH'}`, {
          to: await underlying.getAddress(),
          data: underlying.interface.encodeFunctionData('mint', [state.account, uAmount * 2n - balance]),
        });
      }
      const allowance = await underlying.allowance(state.account, wrapperAddress);
      if (allowance < uAmount) {
        await sendTx(`approve ${which === 'A' ? 'vUSD' : 'vETH'}`, {
          to: await underlying.getAddress(),
          data: underlying.interface.encodeFunctionData('approve', [wrapperAddress, uAmount * 10n]),
        });
      }
      await sendTx(`wrap ${which === 'A' ? 'vUSD' : 'vETH'}`, {
        to: wrapperAddress,
        data: wrapper.interface.encodeFunctionData('wrap', [state.account, uAmount]),
      });
      const enc = await getFhevm().then((i) => i.createEncryptedInput(wrapperAddress, state.account).add64(cAmount).encrypt());
      await sendTxWithInputRetry('fund vault', {
        to: wrapperAddress,
        data: wrapper.interface.encodeFunctionData('confidentialTransferAndCall', [config.batch, enc.handles[0], enc.inputProof, '0x00']),
      });
      appendEngineLog('flow', `vault funded (public funding leg — amount visible by design)`);
    }
    
    /** Exit: unwrap the whole vault balance of one asset to plain tokens. */
    async function exitVault(asset) {
      const config = requireConfig();
      const have = state.funded[asset];
      if (!state.account) { setStatus('Connect wallet first.', 'err'); return; }
      if (!have || have === 0n) { setStatus('Nothing in the vault for this token.', 'err'); return; }
      try {
        setStatus('Requesting withdrawal…');
        const instance = await getFhevm();
        const enc = await instance.createEncryptedInput(config.batch, state.account).add64(have).encrypt();
        await sendTxWithInputRetry('withdraw', {
          to: config.batch,
          data: batchContract(await writeProvider().getSigner()).interface.encodeFunctionData('exit', [asset, enc.handles[0], enc.inputProof]),
        });
        setStatus('Withdrawal queued — plain tokens arrive after the unwrap finalizes (a minute or two).', 'ok');
        await refreshAll();
      } catch (error) {
        setStatus(String(error.message?.shortMessage || error.message).slice(0, 160), 'err');
      }
    }
    
    // ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    // Wire-up
    // ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    el('connectBtn').addEventListener('click', () => connectWallet().catch((e) => setStatus(String(e.message).slice(0, 160), 'err')));
    el('swapBtn').addEventListener('click', privateSwap);
    el('exitA').addEventListener('click', () => exitVault(0));
    el('exitB').addEventListener('click', () => exitVault(1));
    el('dirAB').addEventListener('click', () => { state.direction = 1; el('dirAB').classList.add('on'); el('dirBA').classList.remove('on'); });
    el('dirBA').addEventListener('click', () => { state.direction = 2; el('dirBA').classList.add('on'); el('dirAB').classList.remove('on'); });
    
    loadConfig()
      .then(() => { if (state.config?.compatible) setStatus('Connect your wallet to swap privately.'); })
      .catch((error) => setStatus(String(error.message).slice(0, 160), 'err'));
    appendEngineLog('boot', 'Veil minimal UI loaded');

    const ICONS = {
      receive: "M12 4v12m0 0-4-4m4 4 4-4M5 20h14",
      send: "M7 17 17 7m0 0H8m9 0v9",
      shield: "M12 3 5 6v5c0 4.5 3 8 7 10 4-2 7-5.5 7-10V6l-7-3Zm-3 9 2 2 4-4",
      withdraw: "M12 20V8m0 0-4 4m4-4 4 4M5 4h14",
      refresh: "M20 11a8 8 0 1 0-2.3 5.7M20 4v7h-7",
      lock: "M7 11V8a5 5 0 0 1 10 0v3M6 11h12v9H6z",
      copy: "M9 9h10v11H9zM5 15V4h10",
      qr: "M4 4h6v6H4zM14 4h6v6h-6zM4 14h6v6H4zM14 14h2v2h-2zM18 14h2v6h-4M14 18h2",
      wallet: "M4 7h15a1 1 0 0 1 1 1v10a1 1 0 0 1-1 1H5a2 2 0 0 1-2-2V6a2 2 0 0 1 2-2h12M16 13h2",
      offline: "M12 19h.01M8.5 15.5a5 5 0 0 1 7 0M5 12a10 10 0 0 1 4-2.4M19 12a10 10 0 0 0-3.3-2.2M3 3l18 18",
      settings: "M4 7h9m4 0h3M4 17h3m4 0h9M15 4v6M9 14v6",
      close: "M6 6l12 12M18 6 6 18",
      check: "M5 12.500 10 17.500 19 7",
      chevron: "m9 6 6 6-6 6",
      activity: "M4 12h4l3-8 4 16 3-8h2",
    } as const;
    export type IconName = keyof typeof ICONS;
    
    export function Icon({ name, className = "h-5 w-5" }: { name: IconName; className?: string }) {
      return (
        <svg viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" strokeWidth="1.8" strokeLinecap="round" strokeLinejoin="round" className={className} aria-hidden="true">
          <path d={ICONS[name]} />
        </svg>
      );
    }
    
    /** The Prawl mark: a shielded drop. */
    export function Logo({ className = "h-7 w-7" }: { className?: string }) {
      return (
        <svg viewBox="0 0 32 32" className={className} aria-hidden="true">
          <defs>
            <linearGradient id="prawl-g" x1="0" y1="0" x2="1" y2="1">
              <stop offset="0" stopColor="#34d399" />
              <stop offset="1" stopColor="#059669" />
            </linearGradient>
          </defs>
          <path d="M16 2.500 5 7v8c0 7 4.700 11.800 11 14.500C22.300 26.800 27 22 27 15V7L16 2.500Z" fill="url(#prawl-g)" />
          <path d="M16 9v9.500M12 15l4 4 4-4" stroke="#022c22" strokeWidth="2.200" strokeLinecap="round" strokeLinejoin="round" fill="none" />
        </svg>
      );
    }
    
    /** Ether diamond in a round badge. */
    export function EthIcon({ className = "h-10 w-10" }: { className?: string }) {
      return (
        <span className={`inline-flex shrink-0 items-center justify-center rounded-full bg-zinc-800 ${className}`}>
          <svg viewBox="0 0 24 24" className="h-1/2 w-1/2" aria-hidden="true">
            <path d="M12 2 5.500 12.200 12 16l6.500-3.800L12 2Z" fill="#a1a1aa" />
            <path d="M12 17.200 5.500 13.400 12 22l6.500-8.600-6.500 3.800Z" fill="#71717a" />
          </svg>
        </span>
      );
    }
    
    function hash(s: string): number {
      let h = 2166136261;
      for (let i = 0; i < s.length; i++) h = Math.imul(h ^ s.charCodeAt(i), 16777619);
      return h >>> 0;
    }
    
    /** A round gradient avatar derived from an address (deterministic, like a blockies/jazzicon). */
    export function Identicon({ seed, className = "h-8 w-8" }: { seed: string; className?: string }) {
      const h = hash(seed.toLowerCase());
      const a = h % 360;
      const b = (a + 60 + ((h >> 9) % 140)) % 360;
      const angle = (h >> 17) % 360;
      return (
        <span
          aria-hidden="true"
          className={`inline-block shrink-0 rounded-full ${className}`}
          style={{ background: `linear-gradient(${angle}deg, hsl(${a} 70% 55%), hsl(${b} 75% 45%))` }}
        />
      );
    }
    
    /** A round action button with a label under it (Receive, Shield, Send, Withdraw). */
    export function ActionButton({ icon, label, onClick, disabled }: { icon: IconName; label: string; onClick: () => void; disabled?: boolean }) {
      return (
        <button onClick={onClick} disabled={disabled} className="group flex w-16 flex-col items-center gap-1.5 text-xs text-zinc-300 disabled:opacity-40">
          <span className="flex h-12 w-12 items-center justify-center rounded-full bg-emerald-500 text-zinc-950 transition group-hover:bg-emerald-400 group-active:scale-95">
            <Icon name={icon} className="h-5 w-5" />
          </span>
          {label}
        </button>
      );
    }

    /**
     * Public transactions from the wallet's EVM account, entirely over Anon-RPC.
     * Every read and the broadcast use the account's own identity (its own Tor
     * circuit); waiting for the receipt uses the transaction's own identity.
     * Signing happens here, in memory; the key never leaves this tab.
     */
    import { AnonRpcError, type AnonRpcClient } from "@prawl/anon-rpc";
    import type { Call, Hex } from "@prawl/railgun";
    import { privateKeyToAccount } from "viem/accounts";
    
    export interface ReceiptLog {
      address: Hex;
      topics: Hex[];
      data: Hex;
    }
    
    export interface SentTx {
      hash: Hex;
      status: "success" | "reverted";
      blockNumber: bigint;
      gasUsed: bigint;
      /** Logs of the mined transaction, for callers whose outcome is not just the status (e.g. a redeem that returns false). */
      logs?: ReceiptLog[];
    }
    
    const hexBig = (v: unknown): bigint => BigInt(v as string);
    
    /**
     * A submission whose outcome we cannot tell: the answer never came back, the
     * transaction was not seen mined in time, or the bundler returned another hash.
     * It may still land, so callers must say "status unknown" rather than "failed",
     * and must not offer a blind retry (a retry could pay twice).
     */
    export class UnconfirmedError extends Error {
      override name = "UnconfirmedError";
      constructor(
        message: string,
        readonly hash?: Hex,
      ) {
        super(message);
      }
    }
    
    /** A JSON-RPC error answer means the node or bundler refused it: safe to say it was not sent. */
    const refused = (e: unknown): boolean => e instanceof AnonRpcError && e.code === "RPC_ERROR";
    
    /** ETH with up to 6 decimals, rounded down. */
    function eth(wei: bigint): string {
      const s = (wei < 0n ? 0n : wei).toString().padStart(19, "0");
      return `${s.slice(0, -18)}.${s.slice(-18, -12)}`.replace(/\.?0+$/, "") || "0";
    }
    
    /** Thrown before signing when value plus the worst-case gas cost exceeds the balance. */
    export class InsufficientFundsError extends Error {
      constructor(
        readonly balance: bigint,
        readonly value: bigint,
        readonly maxGasCost: bigint,
      ) {
        super(
          value > 0n && balance > maxGasCost
            ? `Not enough ETH for gas: this needs up to ${eth(maxGasCost)} ETH of gas on top. Try at most ${eth(balance - maxGasCost)} ETH.`
            : `Not enough ETH: have ${eth(balance)}, need ${eth(value + maxGasCost)} including gas.`,
        );
        this.name = "InsufficientFundsError";
      }
    }
    
    function unwrap(item: { result: unknown } | { error: Error } | undefined, what: string): unknown {
      if (!item) throw new Error(`${what}: no answer`);
      if ("error" in item) throw new Error(`${what}: ${item.error.message}`);
      return item.result;
    }
    
    /** Sign and send calls one after another (each waits for its receipt). */
    export async function sendCalls(
      client: AnonRpcClient,
      chainId: number,
      privateKey: Hex,
      calls: readonly Call[],
      onStatus?: (s: string) => void,
    ): Promise<SentTx[]> {
      const account = privateKeyToAccount(privateKey);
      const id = client.identities.account(account.address);
      const out: SentTx[] = [];
      for (const [i, call] of calls.entries()) {
        const label = calls.length > 1 ? ` ${i + 1}/${calls.length}` : "";
        onStatus?.(`Preparing transaction${label}…`);
        const txReq = { from: account.address, to: call.to, data: call.data, value: `0x${call.value.toString(16)}` };
        const [nonceR, feeR, tipR, gasR, balR] = await client.batch(id, chainId, [
          { method: "eth_getTransactionCount", params: [account.address, "pending"] },
          { method: "eth_getBlockByNumber", params: ["latest", false] },
          { method: "eth_maxPriorityFeePerGas", params: [] },
          { method: "eth_estimateGas", params: [txReq, "latest"] },
          { method: "eth_getBalance", params: [account.address, "latest"] },
        ]);
        const nonce = Number(hexBig(unwrap(nonceR, "nonce")));
        const baseFee = hexBig((unwrap(feeR, "latest block") as { baseFeePerGas: string }).baseFeePerGas);
        const tip = hexBig(unwrap(tipR, "priority fee"));
        const gas = (hexBig(unwrap(gasR, "gas estimate")) * 12n) / 10n;
        const maxFeePerGas = baseFee * 2n + tip;
        const balance = hexBig(unwrap(balR, "balance"));
        if (call.value + gas * maxFeePerGas > balance) throw new InsufficientFundsError(balance, call.value, gas * maxFeePerGas);
        const raw = await account.signTransaction({
          type: "eip1559",
          chainId,
          nonce,
          to: call.to,
          data: call.data,
          value: call.value,
          gas,
          maxPriorityFeePerGas: tip,
          maxFeePerGas,
        });
        onStatus?.(`Sending transaction${label} over Tor…`);
        let hash: Hex;
        try {
          hash = (await client.write(id, chainId, "eth_sendRawTransaction", [raw])) as Hex;
        } catch (e) {
          if (refused(e)) throw e;
          throw new UnconfirmedError(`no answer when sending the transaction (${e instanceof Error ? e.message : String(e)}); it may still be mined`);
        }
        onStatus?.(`Waiting for transaction${label} to be mined…`);
        out.push(await waitForReceipt(client, chainId, hash));
        if (out.at(-1)!.status !== "success") break;
      }
      return out;
    }
    
    export async function waitForReceipt(client: AnonRpcClient, chainId: number, hash: Hex, timeoutMs = 300_000): Promise<SentTx> {
      const id = client.identities.tx(hash);
      const deadline = Date.now() + timeoutMs;
      while (Date.now() < deadline) {
        const r = (await client.read(id, chainId, "eth_getTransactionReceipt", [hash]).catch(() => null)) as
          | { status: string; blockNumber: string; gasUsed: string; logs?: ReceiptLog[] }
          | null;
        if (r) {
          return {
            hash,
            status: r.status === "0x1" ? "success" : "reverted",
            blockNumber: hexBig(r.blockNumber),
            gasUsed: hexBig(r.gasUsed),
            logs: (r.logs ?? []).map((l) => ({ address: l.address, topics: l.topics, data: l.data })),
          };
        }
        await new Promise((res) => setTimeout(res, 4000));
      }
      throw new UnconfirmedError(`transaction ${hash} was not seen mined after ${timeoutMs / 1000} s; it may still land`, hash);
    }
    
    /** Public ETH balance of an address, on that address's own circuit. */
    export async function publicBalance(client: AnonRpcClient, chainId: number, address: string): Promise<bigint> {
      return hexBig(await client.read(client.identities.account(address), chainId, "eth_getBalance", [address, "latest"]));
    }
    
    /**
     * Send a signed UserOperation and wait for it. The bundler call names the
     * one-time sender, so it goes on that sender's own identity (fresh circuit);
     * the receipt wait uses the UserOperation hash's identity.
     */
    export async function sendUserOp(
      client: AnonRpcClient,
      chainId: number,
      sender: string,
      signed: unknown,
      expectedHash: Hex,
      onStatus?: (s: string) => void,
      timeoutMs = 300_000,
    ): Promise<SentTx> {
      const op = signed as { userOp: unknown; entryPoint: string };
      if (!op?.userOp || !op.entryPoint) throw new Error("malformed signed UserOperation");
      const id = client.identities.account(sender);
      let hash: Hex;
      try {
        hash = (await client.write(id, chainId, "eth_sendUserOperation", [op.userOp, op.entryPoint])) as Hex;
      } catch (e) {
        if (refused(e)) throw e;
        throw new UnconfirmedError(`no answer from the bundler (${e instanceof Error ? e.message : String(e)}); the operation may still be accepted`, expectedHash);
      }
      if (hash.toLowerCase() !== expectedHash.toLowerCase()) {
        throw new UnconfirmedError(`the bundler returned ${hash}, expected ${expectedHash}; the operation may have been accepted`, hash);
      }
      onStatus?.("Waiting for the bundler to include it…");
      const wait = client.identities.tx(hash);
      const deadline = Date.now() + timeoutMs;
      while (Date.now() < deadline) {
        const r = (await client.read(wait, chainId, "eth_getUserOperationReceipt", [hash]).catch(() => null)) as
          | { success: boolean; receipt: { transactionHash: Hex; blockNumber: string; gasUsed: string } }
          | null;
        if (r) {
          return { hash: r.receipt.transactionHash, status: r.success ? "success" : "reverted", blockNumber: hexBig(r.receipt.blockNumber), gasUsed: hexBig(r.receipt.gasUsed) };
        }
        await new Promise((res) => setTimeout(res, 4000));
      }
      throw new UnconfirmedError(`UserOperation ${hash} was not seen included after ${timeoutMs / 1000} s; it may still land`, hash);
    }
    /**
     * The unlocked wallet. Keys come from the passkey's PRF output and live only in
